It was more just hard to leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you had to perform these actions, like starting projects or calling suppliers, in a specific way or according to specific rules?\n\nPatient: Not really specific rules, no. It wasn’t like I had to do it a certain number of times or in a certain order. I just… felt like I needed to deal with it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious about not being able to keep up with your projects and plans?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I guess I don’t like falling behind, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious. It’s just been hard to keep track of everything.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel a strong urge to perform these actions, like starting projects or calling suppliers, to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. It wasn’t like I thought something terrible would happen if I didn’t do it. It just felt… hard to leave alone.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s been affecting work. I had to ask to be pulled out of a shift-planning meeting, and occupational health got involved because I couldn’t really make the basic production decisions I’m supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Did performing these actions, like starting projects or calling suppliers, make you feel less anxious or more in control at the time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like once I saw all these things that needed fixing, I couldn’t just leave them alone. I kept starting more—projects, supplier calls, the shift plan… and then I couldn’t keep up with what I’d started.\n\nDoctor: Did you find yourself thinking about these projects and plans even when you didn't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe. I mean, it was hard to switch off, but I’m not sure if that’s exactly what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Did you have thoughts about the projects and plans that repeated over and over in your mind?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I kept coming back to them, but it didn’t feel like… a phrase looping or anything. It was more just hard to leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you had to perform these actions, like starting projects or calling suppliers, in a specific way or according to specific rules?\n\nPatient: Not really specific rules, no. It wasn’t like I had to do it a certain number of times or in a certain order. I just… felt like I needed to deal with it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious about not being able to keep up with your projects and plans?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I guess I don’t like falling behind, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious. It’s just been hard to keep track of everything.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel a strong urge to perform these actions, like starting projects or calling suppliers, to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. It wasn’t like I thought something terrible would happen if I didn’t do it. It just felt… hard to leave alone.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s been affecting work. I had to ask to be pulled out of a shift-planning meeting, and occupational health got involved because I couldn’t really make the basic production decisions I’m supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Did performing these actions, like starting projects or calling suppliers, make you feel less anxious or more in control at the time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Maybe for a moment it felt like I was doing something useful, but I don’t know if it made me less anxious exactly. It was more just… I kept going with it.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were doing these actions, like starting projects or calling suppliers, to get rid of unwanted thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I didn’t think of it that way at the time… it was more like I just got caught up in doing them.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure. I haven’t been paying that much attention to that side of things, I guess. Nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you had no choice but to continue working on these projects and plans once you started them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. Once I’d started the plant redesign stuff and the supplier calls and the shift plan, it felt hard to just stop. I mean, I kept coming back to them, but it didn’t feel like… a phrase looping or anything. It was more just hard to leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you had to perform these actions, like starting projects or calling suppliers, in a specific way or according to specific rules?\n\nPatient: Not really specific rules, no. It wasn’t like I had to do it a certain number of times or in a certain order. I just… felt like I needed to deal with it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious about not being able to keep up with your projects and plans?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I guess I don’t like falling behind, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious. It’s just been hard to keep track of everything.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel a strong urge to perform these actions, like starting projects or calling suppliers, to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. It wasn’t like I thought something terrible would happen if I didn’t do it. It just felt… hard to leave alone.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s been affecting work. I had to ask to be pulled out of a shift-planning meeting, and occupational health got involved because I couldn’t really make the basic production decisions I’m supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Did performing these actions, like starting projects or calling suppliers, make you feel less anxious or more in control at the time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Maybe for a moment it felt like I was doing something useful, but I don’t know if it made me less anxious exactly. It was more just… I kept going with it.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were doing these actions, like starting projects or calling suppliers, to get rid of unwanted thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I didn’t think of it that way at the time… it was more like I just got caught up in doing them.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure. I haven’t been paying that much attention to that side of things, I guess. Nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you had no choice but to continue working on these projects and plans once you started them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. Once I’d started the plant redesign stuff and the supplier calls and the shift plan, it felt hard to just stop. I haven’t been paying that much attention to that side of things, I guess. Once I’d started the plant redesign stuff and the supplier calls and the shift plan, it felt hard to just stop. I moved into a temporary apartment about three weeks ago, and around then things kind of shifted hard. I went from being really in overdrive to… barely functioning. Maybe for a moment it felt like I was doing something useful, but I don’t know if it made me less anxious exactly. It was more just… I kept going with it.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were doing these actions, like starting projects or calling suppliers, to get rid of unwanted thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I didn’t think of it that way at the time… it was more like I just got caught up in doing them.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure. I haven’t been paying that much attention to that side of things, I guess. Nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you had no choice but to continue working on these projects and plans once you started them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. Once I’d started the plant redesign stuff and the supplier calls and the shift plan, it felt hard to just stop. Like I’d set all these things moving and then I had to keep up with them… until I couldn’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and are difficult to control?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… not in a way I’d call that, I guess. My mind gets busy, but I don’t know if it’s repeating thoughts exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant stress or a major life change around the time your symptoms started?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yeah. I moved into a temporary apartment about three weeks ago, and around then things kind of shifted hard. I went from being really in overdrive to… barely functioning. That’s been going on since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts that you consider unreasonable or excessive that keep coming back to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I get stuck thinking sometimes, but I wouldn’t say there’s one unreasonable thought that keeps coming back like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any intrusive thoughts that you try to ignore or suppress?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nothing that feels like it’s forcing its way in and I’m trying to push it away… not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts that feel like they're intrusive or unwelcome, and that you've been trying to get rid of?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like thoughts I’m trying to get rid of. I have worries, I guess, but not in that way where they feel intrusive or unwanted and keep coming back like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant decrease in your symptoms when you were not thinking about work or the projects?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t really paid attention to whether it changes when I’m not thinking about work.\n\nDoctor: Did your symptoms start shortly after you moved into the temporary apartment?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty much.
